### S4-08 · C723972 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723972
- Title (old = new): Moving three customers to Net 30 keeps their limits and shows a toast
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop moves the three customers on a term of its own to Net 30.

  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 15 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly three customers, {Customer-A}, {Customer-B} and {Customer-C} (a new term holds only this case's customers), whose names are in that A to Z order, with credit limits of $500.00, $0.00 and $1,000.00. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-08 N15, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-08 Alpha, {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-08 Bravo and {Customer-C} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-08 Charlie._
  - Before you start, Net 30's Customers count reads {Count-A} (write it down). _For example: {Count-A} is 40._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 15 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly three customers made for this test (a new term holds only this case's customers): customer A with a credit limit of $500.00, customer C with a credit limit of $1,000.00, and one more customer.
  - Before you start, write down Net 30's Customers count (number A).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.

  - Step 2:
    - A window titled "Move Customers from term A" opens.
  - Step 3:
    - No line about credit limits shows.
  - Step 4:
    - No confirmation opens.
    - The window closes, a green toast shows "3 customers moved to Net 30.", term A's Customers count reads 0 (3 − 3) and Net 30's reads number A + 3 (for example 43).
  - Step 5:
    - The "Credit term" reads Net 30 and the credit limit is still $500.00.
  - Step 6:
    - The "Credit term" reads Net 30 and the credit limit is still $1,000.00.

### S4-20 · C723984 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723984
- Title (old = new): Escape or a click outside keeps the Move window, Cancel closes it
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You start moving customers to Net 45 and then press Escape, click outside or cancel.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Net 30 is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 15 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly three customers, {Customer-A}, {Customer-B} and {Customer-C} (a new term holds only this case's customers), whose names are in that A to Z order, with credit limits of $500.00, $0.00 and $1,000.00. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-20 N15, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-20 Alpha, {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-20 Bravo and {Customer-C} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-20 Charlie._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure Net 30 is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A, due 15 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly three customers made for this test (a new term holds only this case's customers), one of them customer B.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Untick {Customer-B}.  →  Untick customer B.
  4. Choose "Net 45" in "Move To".
  5. Press Escape.
  6. Click on the page outside the window.
  7. Click "Cancel".
  8. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  9. Click "Move Customers".
  10. Click the close icon at the top right of the window.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: a window titled "Move Customers from {Term-A}" opens.
  - Step 3: "2 selected".
  - Step 4: the dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 5: the window stays open with the same ticks and Net 45.
  - Step 6: the window stays open, unchanged.
  - Step 7: the window closes, no toast shows, and the three customers are still on {Term-A}.
  - Step 8: the menu opens.
  - Step 9: the window opens with all three ticked again.
  - Step 10: the window closes without moving anyone.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - A window titled "Move Customers from term A" opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The count reads "2 selected".
  - Step 4:
    - The dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 5:
    - The window stays open with the same ticks and Net 45.
  - Step 6:
    - The window stays open, unchanged.
  - Step 7:
    - The window closes, no toast shows, and the three customers are still on term A.
  - Step 8:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 9:
    - The window opens with all three ticked again.
  - Step 10:
    - The window closes without moving anyone.

### S4-21 · C723985 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723985
- Title (old = new): Long customer and term names wrap in the Move window
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop's terms and one of its customers have very long names, and you move that customer.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} has a 31-character name and is held by customer {Customer-A}, whose name is more than 60 characters long. Its other own term {Term-B} also has a 31-character name. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-21 Long Name TrmA, {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-21 Long Name TrmB and {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-21 A Very Long Customer Name For Wrapping Checks Ltd._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A has a 31-character name and is held by customer A, whose name is more than 60 characters long. Its other own term B also has a 31-character name.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Choose {Term-B} in "Move To".  →  Choose term B in "Move To".
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: a window titled "Move Customers from {Term-A}" opens. The title shows the whole of {Term-A} and the row shows the whole of {Customer-A}, wrapping onto more lines; nothing is cut off or ends in "...".
  - Step 3: the whole of {Term-B} shows in the dropdown, wrapping if needed.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - A window titled "Move Customers from term A" opens.
    - The title shows the whole of term A and the row shows the whole of customer A, wrapping onto more lines.
    - Nothing is cut off or ends in "...".
  - Step 3:
    - The whole of term B shows in the dropdown, wrapping if needed.

### S4-22 · C723986 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723986

  - The shop's own term {Term-A} is held by exactly 2,000 customers. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-22 Bulk and the customers are ZZAUTOTEST S4-22 0001 to ZZAUTOTEST S4-22 2000 (contacts import)._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

    - The full list shows in under 1 second, with "2000 selected" (or "2,000 selected").
  - Step 3:
    - The list scrolls through every customer, ending with the last one in A to Z order.

### S4-24 · C723988 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723988
- Title (old = new): Emptying a deactivated term with Move lets it be deleted
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop switched off a term of its own that one customer still uses, and wants to delete the term.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, is deactivated and held by one customer, {Customer-A}, and by no vendor or invoice. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-24 Old and {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-24 Customer._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, is deactivated and held by one customer, customer A (made for this test), and by no vendor or invoice.
  - Make sure Net 30 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "Inactive" tab.
  2. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  3. Click "Move Customers".
  4. Choose "Net 30" in "Move To".
  5. Click "Move 1 Customer".
  6. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  7. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  8. Click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: {Term-A} is listed.
  - Step 2: the menu shows "Reactivate", "Move Customers" and a greyed "Delete Credit Term".
  - Step 3: the window "Move Customers from {Term-A}" opens with {Customer-A} ticked.
  - Step 4: the dropdown shows Net 30.
  - Step 5: the toast "1 customer moved to Net 30." shows and {Term-A}'s Customers count reads 0.
  - Step 6: "Delete Credit Term" is now available (not greyed).
  - Step 7: the confirmation opens.
  - Step 8: the toast "{Term-A} deleted" shows and the row is gone.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - Term A is listed.
  - Step 2:
    - The menu shows "Reactivate", "Move Customers" and a greyed "Delete Credit Term".
  - Step 3:
    - The window "Move Customers from term A" opens with customer A ticked.
  - Step 4:
    - The dropdown shows Net 30.
  - Step 5:
    - The toast "1 customer moved to Net 30." shows and term A's Customers count reads 0.
  - Step 6:
    - "Delete Credit Term" is now available (not greyed).
  - Step 7:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 8:
    - The toast "term A deleted" shows and the row is gone.

  - Step 4:

### S4-26 · C723990 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723990
- Title (old = new): With only Settings Finance, Move Customers is greyed with the reason
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A user whose custom role has Finance settings but no customer or AP/AR access opens Move Customers.

  - Net 30 is held by at least one customer, and the shop's own term {Term-A} by none. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-26 Fourteen._

  - Step 5:
    - "Move Customers" is greyed here too.
  - Step 6:
    - The hover reads "You need Customers → Create & Edit and View and Manage AP/AR Data to move customers." (the permission reason, not the no-customers reason).

### S4-31 · C723995 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/723995
- Title (old = new): A ticked customer another user moved meanwhile is left alone
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Two Admins work at once; one moves two customers while the other puts one of them on Net 60.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You and a second user, {User-B}, are both signed in with the "Admin" role, in two separate browsers. _For example: {User-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S4 Second Admin._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 20 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly two customers, {Customer-A} and {Customer-B}. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-31 N20, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-31 Alpha and {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S4-31 Bravo._
  - Starting screen: your browser shows the Credit Terms page; {User-B}'s browser shows Edit Customer for {Customer-B}.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 20 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly two customers, customer A and customer B.
  - Make sure Net 45 and Net 60 are active.
  - Create customer A and customer B just for this test, so no earlier test's changes affect them.
  - Start with your browser showing the Credit Terms page and user B's browser showing Edit Customer for customer B.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Choose "Net 45" in "Move To".
  4. In {User-B}'s browser, set Credit Terms to Net 60.  →  In user B's browser, set Credit Terms to Net 60.
  5. In {User-B}'s browser, click Save.  →  In user B's browser, click Save.
  6. In your browser, click "Move 2 Customers".
  7. Open {Customer-B}'s customer page.  →  Open customer B's customer page.
  8. On the Credit Terms page, open the "⋯" page menu.
  9. Click "Audit Log".
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: a window titled "Move Customers from {Term-A}" opens.
  - Step 3: the button reads "Move 2 Customers".
  - Step 4: the dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 5: {Customer-B} is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 6: the toast reads "1 customer moved to Net 45." (only {Customer-A} is counted).
  - Step 7: {Customer-B} is on Net 60 (left alone), with no move note.
  - Step 8: the menu opens.
  - Step 9: the new "Customers moved" row reads "1 customer from {Term-A} to Net 45".
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - A window titled "Move Customers from term A" opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The button reads "Move 2 Customers".
  - Step 4:
    - The dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 5:
    - Customer B is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 6:
    - The toast reads "1 customer moved to Net 45." (only customer A is counted).
  - Step 7:
    - Customer B is on Net 60 (left alone), with no move note.
  - Step 8:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 9:
    - The new "Customers moved" row reads "1 customer from term A to Net 45".

### S5-02 · C724002 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724002
- Title (old = new): The Credit Terms dropdown lists active terms in page order
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has switched off Net 50 and one term of its own, and you pick a term for a new customer.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Net 50 is deactivated and every other standard term is active. The shop's own active terms include {Term-A} (due 10 days after the invoice date) and {Term-B} (due on day 5 of the following month), and its own term {Term-C} is deactivated. Other shop terms (from other tests) may also be offered. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-02 Ten, {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-02 Fifth and {Term-C} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-02 Old._
  - Starting screen: the Customers page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure Net 50 is deactivated and every other standard term is active. The shop's own active terms include term A (due 10 days after the invoice date) and term B (due on day 5 of the following month), and its own term C is deactivated. Other terms of the shop may also be offered.
  - Start on the Customers page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Customer".
  2. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2: it lists the active terms in the page order: COD first (with a "Default" tag), Due on Receipt second, then the Net terms by days with {Term-A} after Net 7 and before Net 15, then Net 15 to Net 120 (Net 50 missing), then the day-of-the-following-month terms with {Term-B} among them, and Credit Hold last, with no "Default" tag. Net 50 and {Term-C} (inactive) are not offered.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - It lists the active terms in the page order: COD first (with a "Default" tag), Due on Receipt second, then the Net terms by days with term A after Net 7 and before Net 15, then Net 15 to Net 120 (Net 50 missing), then the day-of-the-following-month terms with term B among them, and Credit Hold last, with no "Default" tag.
    - Net 50 and term C (inactive) are not offered.

### S5-06 · C724006 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724006
- Title (old = new): Saving New Customer creates the customer with the chosen term and limit
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop adds a new customer on a term of its own with an $800 credit limit.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - The shop has its own active term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-06 Fourteen._
  - A name for the new customer, {Customer-A}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-06 Customer._
  - Starting screen: the Customers page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date.
  - Choose a name for the new customer, customer A.
  - Start on the Customers page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Customer".
  2. Type {Customer-A} in Customer Name.  →  Type the name of customer A in Customer Name.
  3. Choose {Term-A} in Credit Terms.  →  Choose term A in Credit Terms.
  4. Type 800 in Credit Limit.
  5. Click Save.
  6. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2: the field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 3: the dropdown shows {Term-A}.
  - Step 4: Credit Limit reads 800.
  - Step 5: the window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 6: the "Credit term" reads {Term-A} and the credit limit is $800.00. No extra screen was needed to set the term.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 3:
    - The dropdown shows term A.
  - Step 4:
    - Credit Limit reads 800.
  - Step 5:
    - The window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 6:
    - The "Credit term" reads term A and the credit limit is $800.00.
    - No extra screen was needed to set the term.

### S5-09 · C724009 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724009
- Title (old = new): A deactivated term shows as inactive on the customer, plain on invoices

  - Customer {Customer-A} holds Net 60, which is deactivated, and has an invoice {Invoice-A} created after Net 60 was deactivated. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-09 Customer and {Invoice-A} is the invoice of work order S2-15440._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Make sure customer A (made for this test) holds Net 60, which is deactivated, and has an invoice A (made for this test) created after Net 60 was deactivated.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
  2. Open Edit Customer.
  3. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
  4. Press Escape.
  5. Click Save without changing anything.
  6. Open {Invoice-A}'s printed invoice.  →  Open invoice A's printed invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the card's "Credit term" reads "Net 60 (Inactive)".
  - Step 2: Credit Terms shows "Net 60 (Inactive)" selected.
  - Step 3: "Net 60 (Inactive)" is listed after all the active terms (after Credit Hold).
  - Step 4: the dropdown closes, still on Net 60 (Inactive).
  - Step 5: the customer saves and stays on Net 60 (Inactive).
  - Step 6: the terms read "Net 60", without "(Inactive)".
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The card's "Credit term" reads "Net 60 (Inactive)".
  - Step 2:
    - Credit Terms shows "Net 60 (Inactive)" selected.
  - Step 3:
    - "Net 60 (Inactive)" is listed after all the active terms (after Credit Hold).
  - Step 4:
    - The dropdown closes, still on Net 60 (Inactive).
  - Step 5:
    - The customer saves and stays on Net 60 (Inactive).
  - Step 6:
    - The terms read "Net 60", without "(Inactive)".

Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} has an estimate and an invoice. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-13 Long Term Name, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-13 Customer and {Work-order-1} is S2-15440._

  3. Open the work order print of {Work-order-1}.  →  Open the work order print of work order 1.

  - Step 2: the terms show every character of {Term-A}, without clipping.
  - Step 3: the work order print does not show the credit term at all.

  - Step 1:
    - The terms show every character of term A, without clipping or "...".
  - Step 2:
    - The terms show every character of term A, without clipping.
  - Step 3:
    - The work order print does not show the credit term at all.

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the organization uses the legacy invoice design.
  - Make sure work order 1 has an estimate and an invoice, for a customer who holds the shop's own term A, which has a 31-character name.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open the printed estimate of {Work-order-1}.  →  Open the printed estimate of work order 1.
  2. Open the printed invoice of {Work-order-1}.  →  Open the printed invoice of work order 1.
  3. Open the work order print of {Work-order-1}.  →  Open the work order print of work order 1.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the terms show every character of {Term-A}, without clipping or "...".
  - Step 2: the terms show every character of {Term-A}, without clipping.
  - Step 3: the work order print does not show the credit term at all.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The terms show every character of term A, without clipping or "...".
  - Step 2:
    - The terms show every character of term A, without clipping.
  - Step 3:
    - The work order print does not show the credit term at all.

### S5-15 · C724015 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724015
- Title (old = new): Long term names wrap in the dropdown and on the customer card
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has two terms of its own with 31-character names: one the default, one switched off but held by a customer.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} has a 31-character name and is the default; its own term {Term-B} also has a 31-character name, is deactivated and is held by customer {Customer-A}. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-15 Long Default A, {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-15 Long Inactv BB and {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-15 Customer._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A has a 31-character name and is the default; its own term B also has a 31-character name, is deactivated and is held by customer A.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
  2. Open Edit Customer.
  3. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the card shows the whole of "{Term-B} (Inactive)", wrapping if needed; nothing is cut off.
  - Step 2: the selected value shows the whole name and "(Inactive)".
  - Step 3: {Term-A} shows in full followed by its "Default" tag, and "{Term-B} (Inactive)" shows in full; long names wrap.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The card shows the whole of "term B (Inactive)", wrapping if needed.
    - Nothing is cut off.
  - Step 2:
    - The selected value shows the whole name and "(Inactive)".
  - Step 3:
    - Term A shows in full followed by its "Default" tag, and "term B (Inactive)" shows in full.
    - Long names wrap.

### S5-16 · C724016 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724016
- Title (old = new): Without AP/AR Data access, New and Edit Customer show neither field
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A user who can edit customers but has no AP/AR Data access adds and edits a customer.
  - You are signed in as {User-A}, on a custom role, {Role-A}, that has Customers → Create & Edit but not View and Manage AP/AR Data. _For example: {User-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-16 User and {Role-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-16 No APAR._
  - Customer {Customer-A} exists. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-16 Customer._
  - Starting screen: the Customers page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as a user on a custom role that has Customers → Create & Edit but not View and Manage AP/AR Data.
  - Make sure customer A exists.
  - Start on the Customers page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Customer".
  2. Click "Cancel".
  3. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "New Customer" window opens. Neither "Credit Terms" nor "Credit Limit" shows; the window looks as it did before the release.
  - Step 2: the window closes.
  - Step 3: neither "Credit Terms" nor "Credit Limit" shows (as Edit Customer today).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "New Customer" window opens.
    - Neither "Credit Terms" nor "Credit Limit" shows.
    - The window looks as it did before the release.
  - Step 2:
    - The window closes.
  - Step 3:
    - Neither "Credit Terms" nor "Credit Limit" shows (as Edit Customer today).

### S5-17 · C724017 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724017
- Title (old = new): A customer created without AP/AR access gets the default COD and $0
- **Old preconditions**

  - You are signed in as {User-A}, on a custom role, {Role-A}, that has Customers → Create & Edit but not View and Manage AP/AR Data. _For example: {User-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-17 User and {Role-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-17 No APAR._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - A name for the new customer, {Customer-A}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-17 Customer._
  - An Admin can check the result in a second browser.
  - Starting screen: the Customers page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  - Sign in as a user on a custom role that has Customers → Create & Edit but not View and Manage AP/AR Data.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Choose a name for the new customer, customer A.
  - Have an Admin signed in to a second browser, to check the result.
  - Start on the Customers page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  1. Click "New Customer".
  2. Type {Customer-A} in Customer Name.  →  Type the name of customer A in Customer Name.

  - Step 4: Credit Terms reads COD and the customer has a credit limit of $0.00.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:

  - Step 2:
    - The field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 4:
    - Credit Terms reads COD and the customer has a credit limit of $0.00.

### S5-21 · C724021 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724021
- Title (old = new): If the default changes while New Customer is open, the form keeps its term
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Two Admins work at once; one is adding a customer while the other changes the default term.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You and a second user, {User-B}, are both signed in with the "Admin" role, in two separate browsers. _For example: {User-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-21 Second Admin._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - A name for the new customer, {Customer-A}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S5-21 Customer._
  - Starting screen: your browser shows the Customers page; {User-B}'s browser shows the Credit Terms page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Choose a name for the new customer, customer A.
  - Make sure Net 30 is active.
  - Start with your browser showing the Customers page and user B's browser showing the Credit Terms page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Customer".
  2. Type {Customer-A} in Customer Name.  →  Type the name of customer A in Customer Name.
  3. In {User-B}'s browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 30" row.  →  In user B's browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 30" row.
  4. In {User-B}'s browser, click "Make Default for New Customers".  →  In user B's browser, click "Make Default for New Customers".
  5. In {User-B}'s browser, click "Make Default".  →  In user B's browser, click "Make Default".
  6. In your browser, look at Credit Terms.
  7. Click Save.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2: Credit Terms reads COD (Default).
  - Step 3: the menu opens.
  - Step 4: the confirmation opens.
  - Step 5: {User-B} sees "Net 30 is now the default".
  - Step 6: it still reads COD (the form keeps the term it showed).
  - Step 7: the customer is created on COD, the term the form showed.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "New Customer" window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - Credit Terms reads COD (Default).
  - Step 3:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 4:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 5:
    - User B sees "Net 30 is now the default".
  - Step 6:
    - It still reads COD (the form keeps the term it showed).
  - Step 7:
    - The customer is created on COD, the term the form showed.

### S6-01 · C724027 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724027
- Title (old = new): The vendor Credit Terms dropdown lists active terms, with no Default tag
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has its own default term for customers, has switched off Net 50, and you pick a term for a new vendor.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 10 days after the invoice date, is the default for new customers. Its own term {Term-B}, due on day 5 of the following month, is active. Net 50 is deactivated and every other standard term is active. Other shop terms (from other tests) may also be offered. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-01 Ten and {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-01 Fifth._
  - Starting screen: Parts, Vendors.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A, due 10 days after the invoice date, is the default for new customers. Its own term B, due on day 5 of the following month, is active. Net 50 is deactivated and every other standard term is active. Other terms of the shop may also be offered.
  - Start on Parts, Vendors.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Vendor".
  2. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "New Vendor" window opens.
  - Step 2: it lists the active terms in the page order: COD first, Due on Receipt second, then the Net terms by days with {Term-A} after Net 7 and before Net 15, then Net 15 to Net 120 (Net 50 missing), then the day-of-the-following-month terms with {Term-B} among them, and Credit Hold last. No item carries a "Default" tag (not even {Term-A}).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "New Vendor" window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - It lists the active terms in the page order: COD first, Due on Receipt second, then the Net terms by days with term A after Net 7 and before Net 15, then Net 15 to Net 120 (Net 50 missing), then the day-of-the-following-month terms with term B among them, and Credit Hold last.
    - No item carries a "Default" tag (not even term A).

### S6-02 · C724028 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724028

  3. Click Save & Close.

### S6-03 · C724029 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724029
- Title (old = new): A vendor on a deactivated term shows it as inactive until another is picked
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop switched off Net 60 while a vendor was still on it; another vendor is on Net 30.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds Net 60, which is deactivated. Vendor {Vendor-B} holds Net 30. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-03 Vendor A and {Vendor-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-03 Vendor B._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) holds Net 60, which is deactivated. Vendor B (made for this test) holds Net 30.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Vendor-A}'s vendor page.  →  Open vendor A's vendor page.
  2. Open Edit Vendor.
  3. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
  4. Choose "Net 45".
  5. Click Save & Close.
  6. Open Edit Vendor for {Vendor-B}.  →  Open Edit Vendor for vendor B.
  7. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the "Credit term" reads "Net 60 (Inactive)".
  - Step 2: "Net 60 (Inactive)" is selected in Credit Terms.
  - Step 3: "Net 60 (Inactive)" is listed after the active terms.
  - Step 4: the dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 5: the vendor saves on Net 45.
  - Step 6: it reads Net 30.
  - Step 7: Net 60 is not offered to {Vendor-B}.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The "Credit term" reads "Net 60 (Inactive)".
  - Step 2:
    - "Net 60 (Inactive)" is selected in Credit Terms.
  - Step 3:
    - "Net 60 (Inactive)" is listed after the active terms.
  - Step 4:
    - The dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 5:
    - The vendor saves on Net 45.
  - Step 6:
    - It reads Net 30.
  - Step 7:
    - Net 60 is not offered to vendor B.

### S6-04 · C724030 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724030
- Title (old = new): Vendor credit limits stay editable on COD and Credit Hold
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has a COD vendor with a $2,000 credit limit, and you change its limit and term.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds COD with a credit limit of $2,000.00. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-04 Vendor._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) holds COD with a credit limit of $2,000.00.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open Edit Vendor for {Vendor-A}.  →  Open Edit Vendor for vendor A.
  2. Replace the limit with 2500.
  3. Choose "Credit Hold" in Credit Terms.
  4. Click Save & Close.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: Credit Limit reads $2,000.00 and is editable (no lock, no $0.00).
  - Step 2: the limit reads 2500.
  - Step 3: the limit still reads 2500 and stays editable.
  - Step 4: the vendor saves on Credit Hold with a $2,500.00 limit, exactly as before the release.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - Credit Limit reads $2,000.00 and is editable (no lock, no $0.00).
  - Step 2:
    - The limit reads 2500.
  - Step 3:
    - The limit still reads 2500 and stays editable.
  - Step 4:
    - The vendor saves on Credit Hold with a $2,500.00 limit, exactly as before the release.

### S6-05 · C724031 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724031

### S6-07 · C724033 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724033

- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has two terms of its own with 31-character names, and a vendor is on the switched-off one.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} has a 31-character name, is deactivated and is held by vendor {Vendor-A}; its own term {Term-B} also has a 31-character name and is active. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-07 Long Inactv AA, {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-07 Long Active BB and {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S6-07 Vendor._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A has a 31-character name, is deactivated and is held by vendor A; its own term B also has a 31-character name and is active.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Vendor-A}'s vendor page.  →  Open vendor A's vendor page.
  2. Open Edit Vendor.
  3. Open the Credit Terms dropdown.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the card shows the whole of "{Term-A} (Inactive)", wrapping if needed.
  - Step 2: the selected value shows the whole name and "(Inactive)".
  - Step 3: {Term-B} and "{Term-A} (Inactive)" show in full; long names wrap, nothing is cut off.

    - The card shows the whole of "term A (Inactive)", wrapping if needed.
  - Step 2:
    - The selected value shows the whole name and "(Inactive)".
  - Step 3:
    - Term B and "term A (Inactive)" show in full.
    - Long names wrap, nothing is cut off.

### S7-01 · C724035 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724035
- Title (old = new): An invoice keeps its term and due date when the customer is edited
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A Net 30 customer already has an invoice, and you then change the customer to Net 60.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds Net 30 and has an invoice {Invoice-A} dated Oct 5, 2026, due Nov 4, 2026, showing Terms Net 30. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-01 Customer and {Invoice-A} is the invoice of work order S2-15440._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer A (made for this test) holds Net 30 and has an invoice A (made for this test) dated Oct 5, 2026, due Nov 4, 2026, showing Terms Net 30.
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  2. Choose "Net 60" in Credit Terms.
  3. Click Save.
  4. Open {Invoice-A}.  →  Open invoice A.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the window opens.
  - Step 2: the dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 3: the window closes and the customer is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 4: it still shows Terms Net 30 and Due date Nov 4, 2026.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes and the customer is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 4:
    - It still shows Terms Net 30 and Due date Nov 4, 2026.

### S7-01c · C724037 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724037
- Title (old = new): An invoice keeps its term and due date when the customer is moved
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A customer on a term of the shop's own already has an invoice, and you then move the customer to Net 60.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 20 days after the invoice date, is held only by customer {Customer-A}, who has an invoice {Invoice-A} dated Oct 5, 2026, due Oct 25, 2026, showing Terms {Term-A}. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-01c N20, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-01c Customer and {Invoice-A} is the invoice of work order S2-15448._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 20 days after the invoice date, is held only by customer A (made for this test), who has an invoice A (made for this test) dated Oct 5, 2026, due Oct 25, 2026, showing Terms term A.
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Choose "Net 60" in "Move To".
  4. Click "Move 1 Customer".
  5. Open {Invoice-A}.  →  Open invoice A.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the window opens with {Customer-A} ticked.
  - Step 3: the dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 4: the toast "1 customer moved to Net 60." shows.
  - Step 5: it still shows Terms {Term-A} and Due date Oct 25, 2026 (Oct 5 + 20 days).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens with customer A ticked.
  - Step 3:
    - The dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 4:
    - The toast "1 customer moved to Net 60." shows.
  - Step 5:
    - It still shows Terms term A and Due date Oct 25, 2026 (Oct 5 + 20 days).

### S7-02 · C724038 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724038
- Title (old = new): A vendor invoice is due by the vendor's term and recalculated on change
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop enters a bill from a vendor on a term of its own, then changes the bill's date and vendor.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, and vendor {Vendor-B} holds Net 30. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-02 Vendor A, {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-02 Fourteen and {Vendor-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-02 Vendor B._
  - A vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-A} for {Vendor-A}, not yet saved, is open in Parts, Vendors, Vendor Invoices. _For example: {Vendor-invoice-A} is a new vendor invoice for one part._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor B (made for this test) holds Net 30.
  - Make sure a new vendor invoice, not yet saved, is open in Parts, Vendors, Vendor Invoices, for a vendor who holds one of the shop's own terms, due 14 days after the invoice date.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Set the invoice date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Save the vendor invoice.
  3. Change the invoice date to Oct 20, 2026.
  4. Save the vendor invoice.
  5. Change the vendor to {Vendor-B}.  →  Change the vendor to vendor B.
  6. Save the vendor invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the due date shows Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  - Step 2: it is saved with due date Oct 19, 2026.
  - Step 3: the due date is recalculated to Nov 3, 2026 (Oct 20 + 14 days).
  - Step 4: it is saved with due date Nov 3, 2026.
  - Step 5: the due date is recalculated to Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days, Net 30).
  - Step 6: the stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The due date shows Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  - Step 2:
    - It is saved with due date Oct 19, 2026.
  - Step 3:
    - The due date is recalculated to Nov 3, 2026 (Oct 20 + 14 days).
  - Step 4:
    - It is saved with due date Nov 3, 2026.
  - Step 5:
    - The due date is recalculated to Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days, Net 30).
  - Step 6:
    - The stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.

### S7-03 · C724039 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724039
- Title (old = new): The Vendor Bill Export shows the stored due date, not the vendor's new term
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop exports its vendor bills after a vendor's term changed, including an old bill with no due date.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds Net 30 and has a vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-A} dated Oct 5, 2026, stored due Nov 4, 2026. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-03 Vendor and {Vendor-invoice-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-03 Bill 1._
  - Vendor {Vendor-B} has a vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-B} with no stored due date (from before the release). _For example: {Vendor-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-03 Old Vendor._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) holds Net 30 and has a vendor invoice A (made for this test) dated Oct 5, 2026, stored due Nov 4, 2026.
  - Make sure vendor invoice B, for another vendor, has no stored due date (from before the release).
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open Edit Vendor for {Vendor-A}.  →  Open Edit Vendor for vendor A.
  2. Choose "Net 60" in Credit Terms.
  3. Click Save & Close.
  4. Run the Vendor Bill Export for October 2026.
  5. Find {Vendor-invoice-A} and {Vendor-invoice-B} in the export.  →  Find vendor invoice A and vendor invoice B in the export.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the window opens.
  - Step 2: the dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 3: the window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 4: the export downloads.
  - Step 5: {Vendor-invoice-A}'s due date reads Nov 4, 2026 (stored), not Dec 4, 2026 (what Net 60 would give); {Vendor-invoice-B}'s due date is blank.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 4:
    - The export downloads.
  - Step 5:
    - Vendor invoice A's due date reads Nov 4, 2026 (stored), not Dec 4, 2026 (what Net 60 would give).
    - Vendor invoice B's due date is blank.

### S7-04 · C724040 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724040
- Title (old = new): The same invoice shows the same due date to users in other locations
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop works at two locations in different time zones, and users at both open the same invoice.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You are enrolled at {Location-A} and at {Location-B}, which is in another time zone. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919 (Edmonton time) and {Location-B} is in Auckland._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due on day 10 of the following month, and has an invoice {Invoice-A} at {Location-A} dated Oct 5, 2026. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-04 Customer and {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-04 Tenth._
  - A second user, {User-B}, without Settings → Finance, works in {Location-B}. _For example: {User-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-04 Writer on the Service Advisor role._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure you are also enrolled at location B, which is in another time zone.
  - Make sure Invoice A, dated Oct 5, 2026, at the test location, belongs to a customer who holds one of the shop's own terms, due on day 10 of the following month.
  - Make sure a second user, user B, without Settings → Finance, works in location B.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Invoice-A}.  →  Open invoice A.
  2. Switch location to {Location-B}.  →  Switch location to location B.
  3. Open {Invoice-A} again.  →  Open invoice A again.
  4. In {User-B}'s browser, open {Invoice-A}.  →  In user B's browser, open invoice A.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 2: you now work in {Location-B}.
  - Step 3: the due date still reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 4: {User-B} also sees Nov 10, 2026 (the term list loads for every user).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 2:
    - You now work in location B.
  - Step 3:
    - The due date still reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 4:
    - User B also sees Nov 10, 2026 (the term list loads for every user).

### S7-05 · C724041 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724041
- Title (old = new): A term the app does not recognise is dated exactly as before the release
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: On production, a customer with a credit term ShopView does not know, such as Prepaid, is invoiced after the release.
  - You are signed in to production (the live app, app.shopview.com) as a user with the "Admin" role, working in {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Trucks Hill 2 on the production test account._
  - On production, customer {Customer-A} is stored with a credit term text ShopView does not know, {Text-A}, and an invoice {Invoice-A} for it, dated Oct 5, 2026, was created before the release reached production; its due date then, {Date-A}, was written down (with a screenshot). _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-05 Customer, {Text-A} is Prepaid and {Date-A} is Oct 5, 2026._
  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice. _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15441._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in to production (the live app, app.shopview.com) as a user with the "Admin" role, working in the test location.
  - Make sure that on production, a customer is stored with a credit term text ShopView does not know, "Prepaid", and an invoice for it, dated Oct 5, 2026, was created before the release reached production.
  - Have that invoice's due date from before the release written down (date A), with a screenshot.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test), for the same customer, is complete and ready to invoice.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab.  →  Open work order 1's Finance tab.
  2. Set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  3. Click Create Invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the invoice preview shows.
  - Step 2: the due date reads {Date-A}, the same as before the release.
  - Step 3: the invoice is created with due date {Date-A}; no error, no blank.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The invoice preview shows.
  - Step 2:
    - The due date reads date A, the same as before the release.
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice is created with due date A.
    - No error, no blank.

### S7-06 · C724042 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724042
- Title (old = new): A deactivated term still dates new invoices for customers who keep it
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop switched off a term of its own, but a customer still on it has a work order ready to invoice.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, and {Term-A} is deactivated. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-06 Customer and {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-06 Fourteen._
  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done). _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15442._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done), for a customer who holds the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date.
  - Make sure term A is deactivated.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab.  →  Open work order 1's Finance tab.
  2. Set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  3. Click Create Invoice.
  4. Open the printed invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the invoice preview shows.
  - Step 2: the due date reads Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  - Step 3: the invoice is created.
  - Step 4: it shows the plain name {Term-A} (no "(Inactive)") and the due date Oct 19, 2026.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The invoice preview shows.
  - Step 2:
    - The due date reads Oct 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 14 days).
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice is created.
  - Step 4:
    - It shows the plain name term A (no "(Inactive)") and the due date Oct 19, 2026.

### S7-07 · C724043 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724043
- Title (old = new): An invoice made after a reversal takes the customer's term at that moment
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop reverses a Net 30 invoice after the customer moved to Net 60, and invoices it again.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} has invoice {Invoice-A} for work order {Work-order-1}, dated Oct 5, 2026, created on Net 30 (due Nov 4, 2026). The customer has since been moved to Net 60. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-07 Customer and {Work-order-1} is S2-15443._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) has an invoice dated Oct 5, 2026, created on Net 30 (due Nov 4, 2026); its customer has since been moved to Net 60.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, open the invoice's menu.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, open the invoice's menu.
  2. Click Reverse.
  3. Confirm Reverse.
  4. Set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  5. Click Create Invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu shows Reverse.
  - Step 2: the warning opens.
  - Step 3: the invoice is reversed and Create Invoice shows again.
  - Step 4: the due date reads Dec 4, 2026 (Oct 5 + 60 days).
  - Step 5: the new invoice shows Terms Net 60 and due Dec 4, 2026; Nov 4, 2026 does not come back.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu shows Reverse.
  - Step 2:
    - The warning opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice is reversed and Create Invoice shows again.
  - Step 4:
    - The due date reads Dec 4, 2026 (Oct 5 + 60 days).
  - Step 5:
    - The new invoice shows Terms Net 60 and due Dec 4, 2026.
    - Nov 4, 2026 does not come back.

### S7-08 · C724044 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724044
- Title (old = new): Changing the customer on an open work order takes the new customer's term
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A work order ready to invoice is switched to a different customer, who is on another term.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds Net 30 and customer {Customer-B} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due on day 10 of the following month. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-08 First, {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-08 Second and {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-08 Tenth._
  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done). _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15444._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer B (made for this test) holds the shop's own term A (made for this test), due on day 10 of the following month.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done), for a customer who holds Net 30.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}, change the customer to {Customer-B}.  →  On work order 1, change the customer to customer B.
  2. Open the Finance tab.
  3. Set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  4. Click Create Invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the work order now shows {Customer-B}.
  - Step 2: the invoice preview shows.
  - Step 3: the due date reads Nov 10, 2026 ({Term-A}, not Net 30's Nov 4).
  - Step 4: the invoice shows {Term-A} and due Nov 10, 2026.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The work order now shows customer B.
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice preview shows.
  - Step 3:
    - The due date reads Nov 10, 2026 (term A, not Net 30's Nov 4).
  - Step 4:
    - The invoice shows term A and due Nov 10, 2026.

### S7-09 · C724045 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724045
- Title (old = new): A customer on the shop's own term can charge an invoice to account
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A customer on a term of the shop's own, with a $5,000 credit limit, has a work order ready to invoice.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, with a credit limit of $5,000.00. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-09 Customer and {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-09 Fourteen._
  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done). _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15445._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done), for a customer who holds one of the shop's own terms, due 14 days after the invoice date, with a credit limit of $5,000.00.
  - Make sure Net 30 is active.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab.  →  Open work order 1's Finance tab.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Close the payment window without paying.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: Create Invoice is enabled (invoicing is not blocked).
  - Step 2: the invoice is created and the payment window opens. Charge Account is offered, as for a Net 30 customer.
  - Step 3: the invoice stays (it is not removed, unlike a COD invoice).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - Create Invoice is enabled (invoicing is not blocked).
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice is created and the payment window opens.
    - Charge Account is offered, as for a Net 30 customer.
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice stays (it is not removed, unlike a COD invoice).

### S7-10 · C724046 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724046
- Title (old = new): Changing the invoice date after the customer changed term
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A Net 30 customer was invoiced, then moved to Net 60, and you change that invoice's date.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} has invoice {Invoice-A} created on Net 30, dated Oct 5, 2026 (due Nov 4, 2026); the customer has since been moved to Net 60. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S7-10 Customer and {Invoice-A} is the invoice of work order S2-15446._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure Invoice A was created on Net 30, dated Oct 5, 2026 (due Nov 4, 2026); its customer has since been moved to Net 60.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Invoice-A}.  →  Open invoice A.
  2. Change its invoice date to Oct 10, 2026.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: it shows Terms Net 30, due Nov 4, 2026.
  - Step 2: the due date becomes Nov 9, 2026 (Net 30) or Dec 9, 2026 (Net 60), as the PO answers.

  - Step 1:
    - It shows Terms Net 30, due Nov 4, 2026.
  - Step 2:
    - The due date becomes Nov 9, 2026 (Net 30) or Dec 9, 2026 (Net 60), as the PO answers.

### S8-01 · C724047 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724047
- Title (old = new): A shop's Net term reaches QuickBooks as a standard term on first use
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks, and invoices a customer on a new Net term of its own for the first time.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, is not in QuickBooks' Terms list. Customer {Customer-A} holds {Term-A} and work order {Work-order-1} for it is complete. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-01 Fourteen, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-01 Customer and {Work-order-1} is S2-15440._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, is not in QuickBooks' Terms list.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete, for a customer who holds term A.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Wait until the invoice has synced to QuickBooks (it is no longer on the QB Unexported report).
  4. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  5. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the Due date reads Oct 19, 2026.
  - Step 2: the invoice is created.
  - Step 3: the invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 4: a term named exactly {Term-A} now exists, set as a standard term due in 14 days.
  - Step 5: its terms read {Term-A} and its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the same as in ShopView.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The Due date reads Oct 19, 2026.
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice is created.
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 4:
    - A term named exactly term A now exists, set as a standard term due in 14 days.
  - Step 5:
    - Its terms read term A and its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the same as in ShopView.

### S8-02 · C724048 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724048
- Title (old = new): A day-of-the-following-month term reaches QuickBooks as date-driven
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks, and invoices a customer on a new day-of-the-following-month term of its own.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due on day 10 of the following month, is not in QuickBooks. Customer {Customer-A} holds it and work orders {Work-order-1} and {Work-order-2} for it are complete. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-02 Tenth, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-02 Customer and the work orders are S2-15441 and S2-15442._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due on day 10 of the following month, is not in QuickBooks.
  - Make sure work orders 1 and 2 are complete, both for one customer who holds term A.
  - Create work order 1 and work order 2 just for this test, so no earlier test's changes affect them.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. On {Work-order-2}'s Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 28, 2026.  →  On work order 2's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 28, 2026.
  4. Click Create Invoice.
  5. Wait until both invoices have synced to QuickBooks.
  6. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  7. In QuickBooks, open each of the two invoices.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 2: the invoice is created.
  - Step 3: the Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 4: the invoice is created.
  - Step 5: neither invoice is on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 6: {Term-A} exists as a date-driven term, due on day 10 of the following month.
  - Step 7: both read terms {Term-A} and due Nov 10, 2026, matching ShopView.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice is created.
  - Step 3:
    - The Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 4:
    - The invoice is created.
  - Step 5:
    - Neither invoice is on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 6:
    - Term A exists as a date-driven term, due on day 10 of the following month.
  - Step 7:
    - Both read terms term A and due Nov 10, 2026, matching ShopView.

### S8-03 · C724049 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724049
- Title (old = new): A term QuickBooks already has under the same name is used and never changed
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks, which already has a term with the same name but a different rule.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - QuickBooks already has a term named {Term-A}, set as a standard term due in 20 days. In ShopView, {Term-A} is one of the shop's own terms, due 14 days after the invoice date. Customer {Customer-A} holds it and work order {Work-order-1} for it is complete. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-03 Mixed, {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-03 Customer and {Work-order-1} is S2-15443._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure QuickBooks already has a term named term A (made for this test), set as a standard term due in 20 days. In ShopView, term A is one of the shop's own terms, due 14 days after the invoice date.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete, for a customer who holds term A.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Wait until the invoice has synced.
  4. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  5. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the Due date reads Oct 19, 2026 (14 days, ShopView's rule).
  - Step 2: the invoice is created.
  - Step 3: the invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 4: there is still one {Term-A}, still due in 20 days: ShopView did not change it or add a second one.
  - Step 5: its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the date ShopView sent (only the QuickBooks customer's term may differ).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The Due date reads Oct 19, 2026 (14 days, ShopView's rule).
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice is created.
  - Step 3:
    - The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 4:
    - There is still one term A, still due in 20 days: ShopView did not change it or add a second one.
  - Step 5:
    - Its due date is Oct 19, 2026, the date ShopView sent (only the QuickBooks customer's term may differ).

### S8-04 · C724050 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724050
- Title (old = new): A vendor invoice goes to QuickBooks as a bill with its stored due date
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks and sends a vendor bill on a term of its own that QuickBooks does not have yet.

  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, not yet in QuickBooks; a vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-A} for it, dated Oct 5, 2026, is stored due Oct 19, 2026 and not yet sent. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-04 Vendor, {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-04 Fourteen and {Vendor-invoice-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-04 Bill._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure vendor A holds the shop's own term A, due 14 days after the invoice date, not yet in QuickBooks; a vendor invoice A for it, dated Oct 5, 2026, is stored due Oct 19, 2026 and not yet sent.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  1. Send {Vendor-invoice-A} to QuickBooks the way the shop does today.  →  Send vendor invoice A to QuickBooks the way the shop does today.
  2. In QuickBooks, open the bill.
  3. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: it is sent.
  - Step 2: it is a bill for {Vendor-A}, due Oct 19, 2026, with terms {Term-A}.
  - Step 3: {Term-A} was created, as a standard term due in 14 days.

  - Step 1:
    - It is sent.
  - Step 2:
    - It is a bill for vendor A, due Oct 19, 2026, with terms term A.
  - Step 3:
    - Term A was created, as a standard term due in 14 days.

  - Make sure Net 45 is active.

- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  2. Choose "Net 45" in Credit Terms.
  3. Click Save.
  4. In QuickBooks, open the customer {Customer-A}.  →  In QuickBooks, open the customer A.
  5. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, click Create Invoice.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, click Create Invoice.
  6. Wait until the invoice has synced.
  7. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the window opens.
  - Step 2: the dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 3: the window closes and the customer is saved on Net 45.
  - Step 4: its terms still read Net 30.
  - Step 5: the invoice is created on Net 45.
  - Step 6: the invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 7: the invoice carries Net 45 and its own due date, while the QuickBooks customer still reads Net 30.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The dropdown shows Net 45.
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes and the customer is saved on Net 45.
  - Step 4:
    - Its terms still read Net 30.
  - Step 5:
    - The invoice is created on Net 45.
  - Step 6:
    - The invoice is no longer on the QB Unexported report.
  - Step 7:
    - The invoice carries Net 45 and its own due date, while the QuickBooks customer still reads Net 30.

### S8-07 · C724053 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724053
- Title (old = new): Deactivating or reactivating a term sends nothing to QuickBooks
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks and switches off, then back on, a term QuickBooks already has.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} is already in QuickBooks (an invoice used it). _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-07 Used._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test) is already in QuickBooks (an invoice used it).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Deactivate".
  3. In QuickBooks, open the Terms list (Settings, All lists, Terms).
  4. On the Inactive tab, click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  On the Inactive tab, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  5. Click "Reactivate".
  6. In QuickBooks, refresh the Terms list.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the toast "{Term-A} deactivated" shows.
  - Step 3: {Term-A} is still there, unchanged (active in QuickBooks).
  - Step 4: the menu opens.
  - Step 5: the term is active again.
  - Step 6: {Term-A} is unchanged.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The toast "term A deactivated" shows.
  - Step 3:
    - Term A is still there, unchanged (active in QuickBooks).
  - Step 4:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 5:
    - The term is active again.
  - Step 6:
    - Term A is unchanged.

### S8-07b · C724054 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724054
- Title (old = new): Editing or deleting an unused term sends nothing to QuickBooks
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks and edits, then deletes, a term of its own that nobody uses.

  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.

  3. Replace 14 with 21 in the Days box.
  4. Click "Save Changes".
  5. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-B} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term B row.

  - Step 2:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The blue information line shows below the options.
  - Step 4:
    - A green success toast shows.
  - Step 5:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 6:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 7:
    - The toast "term B deleted" shows.
  - Step 8:
    - Term B never appeared.
