### S8-09 · C724056 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724056
- Title (old = new): A QuickBooks refusal lands on the QB Unexported report with the reason
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop is connected to QuickBooks, which refuses an invoice on a term of the shop's own.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds the shop's own term {Term-A}; its invoice will be refused by QuickBooks for a reason the sandbox gives today, and work order {Work-order-1} for it is complete. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-09 Customer with a tax code the sandbox does not accept, {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S8-09 Fourteen and {Work-order-1} is S2-15445._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the test organization is connected to a QuickBooks Online sandbox company, and you can sign in to that company in another tab.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete, for a customer who holds the shop's own term A (made for this test) and whose invoice QuickBooks will refuse for a reason the sandbox gives today.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. On {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab, click Create Invoice.  →  On work order 1's Finance tab, click Create Invoice.
  2. Open the QB Unexported report.
  3. Fix the cause in the sandbox.
  4. Resend the invoice from the QB Unexported report.
  5. In QuickBooks, open the invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the invoice is created; ShopView work goes on (nothing is blocked).
  - Step 2: the invoice is listed with the reason QuickBooks gave.
  - Step 3: the cause is fixed.
  - Step 4: it syncs and leaves the report.
  - Step 5: it shows terms {Term-A}.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The invoice is created.
    - ShopView work goes on (nothing is blocked).
  - Step 2:
    - The invoice is listed with the reason QuickBooks gave.
  - Step 3:
    - The cause is fixed.
  - Step 4:
    - It syncs and leaves the report.
  - Step 5:
    - It shows terms term A.

### S9-01 · C724060 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724060

- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A Net 30 customer with a $500 credit limit owes $600 in unpaid invoices, and you start a new work order for it.

  - Make sure customer A holds Net 30, has a credit limit of $500.00 and unpaid invoices totalling $600.00.
  - Start on the Work Orders page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  - Step 1: the New Work Order window opens.
  - Step 2: the window shows {Customer-A} as the customer.

    - A confirmation opens titled "Confirmation" with the message "Are you sure you want to open a new work order? This customer is over their credit limit." and the buttons "Create" and "Cancel", worded exactly as before the release.

### S10-01 · C724070 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724070
- Title (old = new): A customer imported with a blank credit term gets the shop's default
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop's own term is the default, and you import a new customer whose credit term cell is empty.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, is the default for new customers. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-01 Fourteen._
  - A contacts import file with one new customer, {Customer-A}, whose credit term cell is empty. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-01 Customer._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, is the default for new customers.
  - Have a contacts import file with one new customer, customer A, whose credit term cell is empty.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Import the file through Settings, Data Import, Contacts.
  2. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the import finishes; no row is refused because of its credit term.
  - Step 2: the "Credit term" reads {Term-A}.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The import finishes.
    - No row is refused because of its credit term.
  - Step 2:
    - The "Credit term" reads term A.

- **Old preconditions**

  1. Import the file through Settings, Data Import, Contacts.
  2. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.

    - No row is refused because of its credit term.
  - Step 2:
    - The "Credit term" reads "Prepaid" exactly as sent.
  - Step 3:
    - "Prepaid" shows selected after the active terms, without "(Inactive)".
  - Step 4:
    - Nothing changes.
    - The import showed no new error message.

### S10-05 · C724074 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724074
- Title (old = new): A new customer imported on COD or Credit Hold gets a $0 limit
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You import three new customers with credit limits: one on COD, one on Credit Hold and one on Net 30.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - A contacts import file with three new customers: {Customer-A} on COD with a credit limit of 500, {Customer-B} on Credit Hold with a credit limit of 750, and {Customer-C} on Net 30 with a credit limit of 900. _For example: they are ZZAUTOTEST S10-05 Cash, ZZAUTOTEST S10-05 Hold and ZZAUTOTEST S10-05 Net._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a contacts import file with three new customers: customer A on COD with a credit limit of 500, customer B on Credit Hold with a credit limit of 750, and customer C (made for this test) on Net 30 with a credit limit of 900.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Import the file through Settings, Data Import, Contacts.
  2. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  3. Close it without saving.
  4. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-B}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer B.

  - Step 2: Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled (the 500 in the file was not kept).
  - Step 3: the window closes.
  - Step 4: Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.
  - Step 5: the window closes.
  - Step 6: Credit Limit shows $900.00, as sent (a Net term's limit is handled as before the release).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The import finishes.
    - No row is refused because of its credit term.
  - Step 2:
    - Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled (the 500 in the file was not kept).
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes.
  - Step 4:
    - Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.
  - Step 5:
    - The window closes.
  - Step 6:
    - Credit Limit shows $900.00, as sent (a Net term's limit is handled as before the release).

- **Old results**

### S10-11 · C724080 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724080
- Title (old = new): A customer created through the API without a term gets the default
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop's own term is the default, and you create two customers through the Public API without a credit term.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You have a valid Public API key for the test organization, the Public API documentation from the developers (it gives the request to create, update and read a customer), and a request tool. _For example: the request tool is Postman._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} is the default for new customers. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-11 Fourteen._
  - Names for two new customers, {Customer-A} and {Customer-B}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-11 None and {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-11 Blank._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a valid Public API key for the test organization, the Public API documentation from the developers (it gives the request to create, update and read a customer), and a request tool.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test) is the default for new customers.
  - Choose names for two new customers, customer A and customer B.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Send a Public API request that creates {Customer-A} with no credit term at all.  →  Send a Public API request that creates customer A with no credit term at all.
  2. Send a Public API request that creates {Customer-B} with an empty credit term.  →  Send a Public API request that creates customer B with an empty credit term.
  3. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
  4. Open {Customer-B}'s customer page.  →  Open customer B's customer page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 2: the window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 3: the "Credit term" reads {Term-A}.
  - Step 4: the "Credit term" reads {Term-A}.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 2:
    - The window closes and the new customer is saved.
  - Step 3:
    - The "Credit term" reads term A.
  - Step 4:
    - The "Credit term" reads term A.

### S10-14 · C724083 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724083

  - A name for a new customer, {Customer-A}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-14 New._
  - Customer {Customer-B} exists on Net 30 with a $600.00 limit. _For example: {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S10-14 Existing._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a valid Public API key for the test organization, the Public API documentation from the developers (it gives the request to create, update and read a customer), and a request tool.
  - Choose a name for a new customer, customer A.
  - Create customer B for this test, on Net 30 with a $600.00 limit.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Send a Public API request that creates {Customer-A} with the credit term "COD" and the credit limit 500.  →  Send a Public API request that creates customer A with the credit term "COD" and the credit limit 500.
  2. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  3. Close it without saving.
  4. Send a Public API request that updates {Customer-B} with the credit term "Credit Hold" and the credit limit 600.  →  Send a Public API request that updates customer B with the credit term "Credit Hold" and the credit limit 600.
  5. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-B}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer B.

  - Step 2: Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.

  - Step 4: the request is accepted (no error comes back).
  - Step 5: Credit Terms reads Credit Hold and Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The customer is created (the request is not refused).
  - Step 2:
    - Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.
  - Step 3:
    - The window closes.
  - Step 4:
    - The request is accepted (no error comes back).
  - Step 5:
    - Credit Terms reads Credit Hold and Credit Limit shows $0.00, disabled.

### S11-01 · C724086 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724086
- Title (old = new): An existing shop keeps COD as its default after the release
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop existed before the release and has never changed its default term, and you add a new customer.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The organization existed before the release, and its default has never been changed: the Credit Terms Log holds no "Default changed" row.
  - A name for a new customer, {Customer-A}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-01 Customer._
  - Starting screen: the Settings page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the organization existed before the release, and its default has never been changed: the Credit Terms Log holds no "Default changed" row.
  - Choose a name for a new customer, customer A.
  - Start on the Settings page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "Credit Terms" in the Finance group.
  2. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  3. Click "Audit Log".
  4. Open the Customers page.
  5. Click "New Customer".
  6. Type {Customer-A} in Customer Name.  →  Type the name of customer A in Customer Name.
  7. Click Save.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the page opens (no feature setting had to be switched on); COD carries the "Default" tag.
  - Step 2: the menu opens.
  - Step 3: there is no "Default changed" row, so the default has been COD since the release.
  - Step 4: the page opens.
  - Step 5: Credit Terms is prefilled with COD (Default).
  - Step 6: the field shows {Customer-A}.
  - Step 7: the customer is created on COD, as before the release.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The page opens (no feature setting had to be switched on).
    - COD carries the "Default" tag.
  - Step 2:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 3:
    - There is no "Default changed" row, so the default has been COD since the release.
  - Step 4:
    - The page opens.
  - Step 5:
    - Credit Terms is prefilled with COD (Default).
  - Step 6:
    - The field shows customer A.
  - Step 7:
    - The customer is created on COD, as before the release.

### S11-02 · C724087 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724087
- Title (old = new): No existing customer, vendor or invoice changes at release
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: On production, you compare a customer, vendor, invoice and vendor bill written down before the release with how they look now.

  - Before the release reached production, these were written down on production (with screenshots): customer {Customer-A}'s term and credit limit, vendor {Vendor-A}'s term and credit limit, and invoice {Invoice-A}'s term and due date, and vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-A}'s due date. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-02 Customer on Net 30 with $1,000.00, {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-02 Vendor on Net 45 with $2,000.00, {Invoice-A} is due Nov 4, 2026 and {Vendor-invoice-A} is due Nov 19, 2026._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  - Sign in to production (the live app, app.shopview.com) as a user with the "Admin" role, working in the test location.
  - Have these written down on production from before the release reached production (with screenshots): customer A's term and credit limit, vendor A's term and credit limit, invoice A's term and due date, and vendor invoice A's due date.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
  2. Open {Vendor-A}'s vendor page.  →  Open vendor A's vendor page.
  3. Open {Invoice-A}.  →  Open invoice A.
  4. Open {Vendor-invoice-A}.  →  Open vendor invoice A.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the term and credit limit are exactly as written down.
  - Step 2: the term and credit limit are exactly as written down.
  - Step 3: its term and due date are exactly as written down (never re-dated).
  - Step 4: its due date is exactly as written down.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The term and credit limit are exactly as written down.
  - Step 2:
    - The term and credit limit are exactly as written down.
  - Step 3:
    - Its term and due date are exactly as written down (never re-dated).
  - Step 4:
    - Its due date is exactly as written down.

  - Step 2:
    - It reads "No inactive terms" with a count of 0.

### S11-05 · C724090 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724090
- Title (old = new): COD at invoicing works as before, with no Charge Account
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A COD customer has a work order ready to invoice.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds COD and work order {Work-order-1} for it is complete and ready to invoice. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-05 Cash and {Work-order-1} is S2-15440._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure work order 1 (made for this test) is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done), for a customer who holds COD.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab.  →  Open work order 1's Finance tab.
  2. Click Create Invoice.
  3. Close the payment window without paying.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: Create Invoice is enabled.
  - Step 2: the payment window opens, as before the release. Charge Account is not offered.
  - Step 3: the new invoice is removed again, as before the release; the work order is back to ready to invoice.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - Create Invoice is enabled.
  - Step 2:
    - The payment window opens, as before the release.
    - Charge Account is not offered.
  - Step 3:
    - The new invoice is removed again, as before the release.
    - The work order is back to ready to invoice.

### S11-09 · C724094 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724094
- Title (old = new): An old COD customer moved to Credit Hold gets $0, its limit kept in the note
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A COD customer still has a $1,000 limit from before the release, and you move it to Credit Hold.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds COD and keeps a credit limit of $1,000.00 stored from before the release. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-09 Cash._
  - Your user name is {User-A}. _For example: {User-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-09 Admin._
  - COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer A holds COD and keeps a credit limit of $1,000.00 stored from before the release.
  - Note your user name.
  - Make sure COD is the shop's default for new customers.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "COD" row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Click "Select None".
  4. Type {Customer-A}'s name in "Search customers".  →  Type customer A's name in "Search customers".
  5. Tick {Customer-A}.  →  Tick customer A.
  6. Choose "Credit Hold" in "Move To".
  7. Click "Move 1 Customer".
  8. Click "Set Limits To $0 And Move".
  9. Open {Customer-A}'s customer page.  →  Open customer A's customer page.
  10. Click the "Notes" tab.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the window opens with every COD customer ticked.
  - Step 3: "0 selected".
  - Step 4: only {Customer-A} is listed.
  - Step 5: "1 selected".
  - Step 6: the line "Their credit limit will be set to $0." shows.
  - Step 7: the confirmation "Set 1 customer's credit limit to $0?" opens.
  - Step 8: the toast "1 customer moved to Credit Hold." shows.
  - Step 9: the "Credit term" reads Credit Hold and the credit limit reads $0.00.
  - Step 10: the note reads "Credit term changed from COD to Credit Hold by {User-A} (Move Customers). Credit limit $1,000.00 → $0."
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens with every COD customer ticked.
  - Step 3:
    - The count reads "0 selected".
  - Step 4:
    - Only customer A is listed.
  - Step 5:
    - The count reads "1 selected".
  - Step 6:
    - The line "Their credit limit will be set to $0." shows.
  - Step 7:
    - The confirmation "Set 1 customer's credit limit to $0?" opens.
  - Step 8:
    - The toast "1 customer moved to Credit Hold." shows.
  - Step 9:
    - The "Credit term" reads Credit Hold and the credit limit reads $0.00.
  - Step 10:
    - The note reads "Credit term changed from COD to Credit Hold by your user name (Move Customers). Credit limit $1,000.00 → $0."

### S11-12 · C724097 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724097
- Title (old = new): A credit memo's due date on a statement counts on the location's calendar
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A Net 30 customer at a location with daylight saving time has a credit memo, and you check its statement.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} holds Net 30 at {Location-A}, whose time zone has daylight saving time, and has a credit memo {Credit-memo-A} dated Oct 20, 2026. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S11-12 Customer, {Location-A} is in Edmonton and {Credit-memo-A} is CM-3576._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer A holds Net 30 at the test location, whose time zone has daylight saving time, and has a credit memo dated Oct 20, 2026.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Customer-A}'s statement.  →  Open customer A's statement.
  2. Open the customer's unpaid transactions list.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days; the Nov 1 clock change does not move it). Before the release this screen could show one day less; the later date is the correct one.
  - Step 2: the credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026, the same.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 20 + 30 days; the Nov 1 clock change does not move it).
    - Before the release this screen could show one day less.
    - The later date is the correct one.
  - Step 2:
    - The credit memo's due date reads Nov 19, 2026, the same.

### S12-02 · C724107 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724107

- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop creates a new term due 14 days after the invoice date, and you check the history.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Your user name is {User-A}. _For example: {User-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S12 Admin._
  - A new name {Term-A}. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S12-02 Net 14._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note your user name.
  - Choose a new name for term A (made for this test).
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type {Term-A} in Name.  →  Type the name of term A in Name.
  3. Type 14 in the Days box.
  4. Click "Create Credit Term".
  5. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  6. Click "Audit Log".
- **Old results**

  - Step 2: the field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 3: the blue information line shows below the options.
  - Step 4: the toast "{Term-A} created" shows.
  - Step 5: the menu opens.
  - Step 6: the newest row reads Event "Credit term created", User {User-A}, Details "{Term-A}: 14 days after the invoice date", with today's date and the time.

  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 3:
    - The blue information line shows below the options.
  - Step 4:
    - The toast "term A created" shows.
  - Step 5:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 6:
    - The newest row reads Event "Credit term created", User (your user name), Details "term A: 14 days after the invoice date", with today's date and the time.

### S12-03 · C724108 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724108
- Title (old = new): Renaming a term logs only the name change

  - A new name {Term-B}. _For example: {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S12-03 New._

  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice.
  - Choose a new name for term B (made for this test).

- **Old steps** / **new steps**

  2. Click "Edit".

  - Step 2: the window opens.
  - Step 3: the field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 4: the toast "{Term-B} saved" shows.
  - Step 5: the menu opens.
  - Step 6: the newest row reads Event "Credit term edited" and Details "Name: {Term-A} → {Term-B}", with nothing about Due.

  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 4:
    - The toast "term B saved" shows.
  - Step 5:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 6:
    - The newest row reads Event "Credit term edited" and Details "Name: term A → term B", with nothing about Due.

### S12-10 · C724115 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724115
- Title (old = new): Log rows show the user, and date and time in your location's calendar
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You switch off Net 50, then read the history from two locations in different time zones.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Your user name is {User-A}. _For example: {User-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S12 Admin._
  - You are enrolled at {Location-B}, in a time zone {Hours-A} hours apart from {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-B} is in Toronto and {Hours-A} is 2 (Edmonton to Toronto)._
  - Net 50 is active.
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note your user name.
  - Make sure you are also enrolled at location B, in a time zone a whole number of hours apart from the test location, and note that difference in hours.
  - Make sure Net 50 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 50" row.
  2. Click "Deactivate".
  3. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  4. Click "Audit Log".
  5. Close the log.
  6. Switch location to {Location-B}.  →  Switch location to location B.
  7. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  8. Click "Audit Log".
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: a green success toast shows.
  - Step 3: the menu opens.
  - Step 4: the newest row shows User {User-A} and the date and time of the change as clocks at {Location-A} read them.
  - Step 5: the log closes.
  - Step 6: the page reloads.
  - Step 7: the menu opens.
  - Step 8: the same row now shows the time {Hours-A} hours later (as clocks at {Location-B} read it), and the date changes too if the move crosses midnight.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - A green success toast shows.
  - Step 3:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 4:
    - The newest row shows your user name under User.
    - It shows the date and time of the change as clocks at the test location read them.
  - Step 5:
    - The log closes.
  - Step 6:
    - The page reloads.
  - Step 7:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 8:

    - The date changes too if the move crosses midnight.

  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  2. Click "Audit Log".
  3. Type "default changed" in the search box.
  4. Replace the text with "NET 45".
  5. Replace the text with your user name in lower-case letters.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the "Credit Terms Log" window opens.
  - Step 3: only "Default changed" rows remain.
  - Step 4: only rows whose Event, User or Details contain Net 45 remain.
  - Step 5: only rows whose User is {User-A} remain.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The "Credit Terms Log" window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - Only "Default changed" rows remain.
  - Step 4:
    - Only rows whose Event, User or Details contain Net 45 remain.
  - Step 5:
    - Only rows whose User is your user name remain.

### S12-14 · C724121 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724121
- Title (old = new): A refused action writes no row
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Two Admins work at once, so one Admin's delete is refused, and you check the history.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You and a second user, {User-B}, are both signed in with the "Admin" role, in two separate browsers. _For example: {User-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S12-14 Second Admin._
  - The shop has its own active term {Term-A}, due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST S12-14 Net 14._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page is open in both browsers.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Have a second user, user B, signed in with the "Admin" role in a second browser (you are signed in as an Admin in the first).
  - Have the shop's own active term A (made for this test), due 14 days after the invoice date, held by no customer, vendor or invoice.
  - Start with the Credit Terms page open in both browsers.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. In your browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  In your browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  3. In {User-B}'s browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  In user B's browser, click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  4. In {User-B}'s browser, click "Make Default for New Customers".  →  In user B's browser, click "Make Default for New Customers".
  5. In {User-B}'s browser, click "Make Default".  →  In user B's browser, click "Make Default".
  6. In your browser, click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.
  7. Click "Cancel".
  8. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  9. Click "Audit Log".
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the confirmation opens.
  - Step 3: {User-B}'s menu opens.
  - Step 4: the confirmation opens.
  - Step 5: {User-B} sees "{Term-A} is now the default".
  - Step 6: the alert "This is your default for new customers. Pick another default first." shows.
  - Step 7: the confirmation closes.
  - Step 8: the menu opens.
  - Step 9: the newest row is {User-B}'s "Default changed"; there is no "Credit term deleted" row.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 3:
    - User B's menu opens.
  - Step 4:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 5:
    - User B sees "term A is now the default".
  - Step 6:
    - The alert "This is your default for new customers. Pick another default first." shows.
  - Step 7:
    - The confirmation closes.
  - Step 8:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 9:
    - The newest row is user B's "Default changed"
    - There is no "Credit term deleted" row.

  - A new name {Term-B}, also 31 characters. _For example: {Term-B} is ZZAUTOTEST S12-15 Long Name 456._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test) has a 31-character name.
  - Choose a new name for term B (made for this test), also 31 characters.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  2. Click "Edit".
  3. Replace the Name with {Term-B}.  →  Replace the Name with the name of term B.
  4. Click "Save Changes".
  5. Click the "⋯" page menu beside "New Credit Term".
  6. Click "Audit Log".
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the window opens.
  - Step 3: the field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 4: a green success toast shows.
  - Step 5: the menu opens.
  - Step 6: the Details "Name: {Term-A} → {Term-B}" shows both names in full, wrapping onto more lines; nothing is cut off.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The field shows the name as typed.
  - Step 4:
    - A green success toast shows.
  - Step 5:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 6:
    - The Details "Name: term A → term B" shows both names in full, wrapping onto more lines.
    - Nothing is cut off.

### DATA-01 · C724127 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724127

  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done). _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15440._

- **Old results**

  - Step 2: the Due date reads Nov 4, 2026 (Oct 5 + 30 days: 26 days to Oct 31, then 4 into November).

  - Step 1:
    - The invoice preview shows Invoice Date and Due date.
  - Step 2:
    - The Due date reads Nov 4, 2026 (Oct 5 + 30 days: 26 days to Oct 31, then 4 into November).

  - Work order {Work-order-1} for {Customer-A} is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done). _For example: {Work-order-1} is S2-15440._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure work order 1 is complete and ready to invoice (every part received, review done), for a customer who holds the shop's own term due on day 10 of the following month.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Open {Work-order-1}'s Finance tab.  →  Open work order 1's Finance tab.
  2. Set Invoice Date to Oct 5, 2026.
  3. Set Invoice Date to Nov 12, 2026.
  4. Set Invoice Date to Jan 31, 2027.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the invoice preview shows Invoice Date and Due date.
  - Step 2: the Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 3: the Due date reads Dec 10, 2026 (recalculated from the new date).
  - Step 4: the Due date reads Feb 10, 2027.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The invoice preview shows Invoice Date and Due date.
  - Step 2:
    - The Due date reads Nov 10, 2026.
  - Step 3:
    - The Due date reads Dec 10, 2026 (recalculated from the new date).
  - Step 4:
    - The Due date reads Feb 10, 2027.

  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

### DATA-17 · C724144 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724144
- Title (old = new): A vendor on Credit Hold has vendor invoices due on the invoice date
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop enters a bill from a vendor on Credit Hold, then switches the bill to a Net 45 vendor.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Vendor {Vendor-A} holds Credit Hold and vendor {Vendor-B} holds Net 45. _For example: {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-17 Held and {Vendor-B} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-17 Forty Five._
  - A new vendor invoice {Vendor-invoice-A} for {Vendor-A} is open, not yet saved. _For example: {Vendor-invoice-A} is a vendor invoice for one part._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor B (made for this test) holds Net 45.
  - Make sure a new vendor invoice is open, not yet saved, for a vendor who holds Credit Hold.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Set the invoice date to Oct 5, 2026.
  2. Change the vendor to {Vendor-B}.  →  Change the vendor to vendor B.
  3. Save the vendor invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the due date reads Oct 5, 2026 (Credit Hold: due on the invoice date).
  - Step 2: the due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 45 days: 26 to Oct 31, then 19).
  - Step 3: the stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The due date reads Oct 5, 2026 (Credit Hold: due on the invoice date).
  - Step 2:
    - The due date reads Nov 19, 2026 (Oct 5 + 45 days: 26 to Oct 31, then 19).
  - Step 3:
    - The stored due date is Nov 19, 2026.

### DATA-18 · C724145 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724145
- Title (old = new): A vendor invoice from a delivery uses the delivery's location calendar
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You receive a delivery that belongs to a location in another time zone, where it is already the next day.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - You are working in {Location-A}; a delivery for vendor {Vendor-A} belongs to {Location-B}, in a time zone where the date is already one day later. Run the case at a time of day when the two dates differ. _For example: {Location-A} is in Edmonton, {Location-B} is in Auckland and {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-18 Vendor on Net 30._
  - Today's date at {Location-B} is {Date-A}; {Date-B} is {Date-A} plus 30 days. _For example: {Date-A} is Oct 22, 2026 and {Date-B} is Nov 21, 2026._
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) holds Net 30.
  - Make sure a delivery for vendor A belongs to location B, in a time zone where the date is already one day later than where you work. Run the case at a time of day when the two dates differ.
  - Note today's date at location B (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 30 days.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Receive the delivery for {Vendor-A}.  →  Receive the delivery for vendor A.
  2. Open the new vendor invoice.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the delivery is received and its vendor invoice is created.
  - Step 2: its due date reads {Date-B}, counted from {Location-B}'s date {Date-A} (not from your location's date).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The delivery is received and its vendor invoice is created.
  - Step 2:
    - Its due date reads date B, counted from location B's date A (not from your location's date).

### DATA-19 · C724146 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724146
- Title (old = new): The blue line's due date is exact for Net 365 and day 31
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You start new terms due 365 days after the invoice date and on the 31st of the following month.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Today's date at {Location-A} is {Date-A}; {Date-B} is {Date-A} plus 365 days, and {Date-C} is day 31 of the month after {Date-A}, or that month's last day if it is shorter. _For example: {Date-A} is Oct 23, {Date-B} is Oct 23 (of next year) and {Date-C} is Nov 30._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note today's date at the test location (date A).
  - Work out date B: date A plus 365 days.
  - Work out date C: day 31 of the month after date A, or that month's last day if it is shorter.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type 365 in the Days box.
  3. Click the Day box.
  4. Type 31 in the Day box.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the window opens.
  - Step 2: the blue line ends "An invoice dated {Date-A} is due {Date-B}."
  - Step 3: the "Due day of the following month" option is selected.
  - Step 4: the blue line ends "An invoice dated {Date-A} is due {Date-C}." (for example Oct 23 → Nov 30, because November has 30 days).
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The blue line ends "An invoice dated date A is due date B."
  - Step 3:
    - The "Due day of the following month" option is selected.
  - Step 4:
    - The blue line ends "An invoice dated date A is due date C." (for example Oct 23 → Nov 30, because November has 30 days).

### DATA-20 · C724147 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724147
- Title (old = new): Customers counts include odd spellings and every status, never Counter Sale
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop imports customers whose terms are spelled oddly, then marks one inactive, and you check the counts.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A} is held by no customer, and Net 30 is held by {Count-B} customers, written down before you start (the Counter Sale customer is never counted). _For example: {Count-B} is 40 and {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-20 Fourteen._
  - A contacts import file with three new customers: {Customer-B} with the credit term text "NET 30", {Customer-C} with "net30" and {Customer-D} with {Term-A} written in capital letters. _For example: {Customer-B}, {Customer-C} and {Customer-D} are ZZAUTOTEST DATA-20 Bravo, Charlie and Delta._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A (made for this test) is held by no customer.
  - Before you start, write down how many customers hold Net 30 (number B; the Counter Sale customer is never counted).
  - Have a contacts import file with three new customers: customer B with the credit term text "NET 30", one with "net30" and one with term A written in capital letters.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Import the file through Settings, Data Import, Contacts.
  2. Reload the Credit Terms page.
  3. Set {Customer-B}'s status to inactive on its customer page.  →  Set customer B's status to inactive on its customer page.
  4. Reload the Credit Terms page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the three customers are created (none is refused).
  - Step 2: Net 30 reads {Count-B} + 2 (for example 40 + 2 = 42): "NET 30" and "net30" both count as Net 30; {Term-A} reads 1.
  - Step 3: the customer is inactive.
  - Step 4: Net 30 still reads {Count-B} + 2: customers count whatever their status.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The three customers are created (none is refused).
  - Step 2:
    - Net 30 reads number B + 2 (for example 40 + 2 = 42): "NET 30" and "net30" both count as Net 30.
    - Term A reads 1.
  - Step 3:
    - The customer is inactive.
  - Step 4:
    - Net 30 still reads number B + 2: customers count whatever their status.

### DATA-21 · C724148 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724148
- Title (old = new): A customer and a vendor stored with a blank term count under COD
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: A customer and a vendor stored before the release with no credit term are counted, and you then change the customer.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Customer {Customer-A} and vendor {Vendor-A} were stored with a blank credit term before the release; COD's Customers count is {Count-A} and Vendors count {Count-B} with them included. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-21 Blank, {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-21 Blank Vendor, {Count-A} is 121 and {Count-B} is 768._
  - The organization's Counter Sale customer is on COD.
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure customer A and a vendor were stored with a blank credit term before the release.
  - Before you start, write down COD's Customers count (number A) and Vendors count (number B), with them included.
  - Make sure the organization's Counter Sale customer is on COD.
  - Make sure Net 30 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Look at the COD row.
  2. Open Edit Customer for {Customer-A}.  →  Open Edit Customer for customer A.
  3. Choose "Net 30" in Credit Terms.
  4. Click Save.
  5. Reload the Credit Terms page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: Customers reads {Count-A} and Vendors reads {Count-B}.
  - Step 2: the window opens.
  - Step 3: the dropdown shows Net 30.
  - Step 4: the window closes and the customer is saved on Net 30.
  - Step 5: COD reads {Count-A} − 1 (for example 121 − 1 = 120). The Counter Sale customer was never in the count.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - Customers reads number A and Vendors reads number B.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The dropdown shows Net 30.
  - Step 4:
    - The window closes and the customer is saved on Net 30.
  - Step 5:
    - COD reads number A − 1 (for example 121 − 1 = 120).
    - The Counter Sale customer was never in the count.

### DATA-22 · C724150 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724150
- Title (old = new): Vendors counts follow the same rules as customers
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Net 45 is held by vendors, one stored as "NET 45" and one inactive, and you move one vendor to Net 60.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Net 45 is held by {Count-A} vendors (written down). Vendor {Vendor-A} is stored with "NET 45" and vendor {Vendor-B} holds Net 45 and is inactive; both are in {Count-A}. _For example: {Count-A} is 5, {Vendor-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-22 Upper and {Vendor-B} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-22 Inactive._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure vendor A (made for this test) is stored with "NET 45" and another vendor holds Net 45 and is inactive.
  - Before you start, write down how many vendors hold Net 45 (number A, which includes vendor A and the inactive vendor).
  - Make sure Net 60 is active.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Look at the Net 45 row's Vendors cell.
  2. Open Edit Vendor for {Vendor-A}.  →  Open Edit Vendor for vendor A.
  3. Choose "Net 60".
  4. Click Save & Close.
  5. Reload the Credit Terms page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: it reads {Count-A} (for example 5): "NET 45" counts as Net 45 and the inactive vendor counts.
  - Step 2: the window opens.
  - Step 3: the dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 4: the window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 5: Net 45 reads {Count-A} − 1 (for example 4) and Net 60's Vendors count went up by 1.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - It reads number A (for example 5): "NET 45" counts as Net 45 and the inactive vendor counts.
  - Step 2:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 3:
    - The dropdown shows Net 60.
  - Step 4:
    - The window closes and the vendor is saved on Net 60.
  - Step 5:
    - Net 45 reads number A − 1 (for example 4) and Net 60's Vendors count went up by 1.

### DATA-23 · C724151 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724151
- Title (old = new): Customers counts update straight after a move
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop moves three Net 15 customers to Net 20, and you check both counts.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Net 15 is held by {Count-A} customers and Net 20 by {Count-B}, written down; three of the Net 15 customers are {Customer-A}, {Customer-B} and {Customer-C}. _For example: {Count-A} is 7, {Count-B} is 2 and the three are ZZAUTOTEST DATA-23 Alpha to Charlie._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure three of the customers on Net 15 are customer A, customer B and customer C.
  - Before you start, write down how many customers hold Net 15 (number A) and how many hold Net 20 (number B).
  - Create customer A, customer B and customer C just for this test, so no earlier test's changes affect them.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 15" row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Click "Select None".
  4. Tick {Customer-A}.  →  Tick customer A.
  5. Tick {Customer-B}.  →  Tick customer B.
  6. Tick {Customer-C}.  →  Tick customer C.
  7. Choose "Net 20" in "Move To".
  8. Click "Move 3 Customers".
  9. Reload the page.
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the menu opens.
  - Step 2: the window shows "{Count-A} selected" (for example "7 selected").
  - Step 3: "0 selected".
  - Step 4: "1 selected".
  - Step 5: "2 selected".
  - Step 6: "3 selected"; the button reads "Move 3 Customers".
  - Step 7: the dropdown shows Net 20.
  - Step 8: the toast reads "3 customers moved to Net 20." and, without reloading, Net 15 reads {Count-A} − 3 (for example 4) and Net 20 reads {Count-B} + 3 (for example 5).
  - Step 9: the counts are the same after the reload.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The window shows "number A selected" (for example "7 selected").
  - Step 3:
    - The count reads "0 selected".
  - Step 4:
    - The count reads "1 selected".
  - Step 5:
    - The count reads "2 selected".
  - Step 6:
    - The count reads "3 selected".
    - The button reads "Move 3 Customers".
  - Step 7:
    - The dropdown shows Net 20.
  - Step 8:
    - The toast reads "3 customers moved to Net 20." and, without reloading, Net 15 reads number A − 3 (for example 4) and Net 20 reads number B + 3 (for example 5).
  - Step 9:
    - The counts are the same after the reload.

### DATA-24 · C724152 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724152
- Title (old = new): Tab counts change by one on each deactivate, reactivate, create and delete
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop creates, switches off, switches on and deletes terms, and you watch the tab counts.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Net 45 is active.
  - Before you start, the Active tab's count reads {Count-A} and the Inactive tab's {Count-B} (write them down). _For example: {Count-A} is 12 and {Count-B} is 0._
  - A name {Term-A} that no term has. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-24 Fourteen._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure Net 45 is active.
  - Before you start, write down the Active tab's count (number A) and the Inactive tab's count (number B).
  - Choose a name for term A that no term has.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  2. Type {Term-A} in Name.  →  Type the name of term A in Name.

  8. Click "Deactivate".

  10. Click the "⋯" at the right of the "Net 45" row.
  11. Click "Reactivate".
  12. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.
  13. Click "Delete Credit Term".
  14. Click the red "Delete Credit Term" button.

  - Step 1: the window opens.
  - Step 2: the field shows {Term-A}.
  - Step 3: the blue information line shows.
  - Step 4: Active reads {Count-A} + 1 (for example 13) and Inactive {Count-B}.
  - Step 5: the menu opens.
  - Step 6: Active reads {Count-A} (back to the starting number) and Inactive {Count-B} + 1.
  - Step 7: the menu opens.
  - Step 8: Active {Count-A} − 1, Inactive {Count-B} + 2.
  - Step 9: Net 45 and {Term-A} are among its rows.
  - Step 10: the menu opens.
  - Step 11: Active {Count-A}, Inactive {Count-B} + 1.
  - Step 12: the menu opens.
  - Step 13: the confirmation opens.
  - Step 14: Active {Count-A} and Inactive {Count-B}: both counts are back where they started (for example 12 and 0).

  - Step 1:
    - The window opens.
  - Step 2:
    - The field shows term A.
  - Step 3:
    - The blue information line shows.
  - Step 4:
    - Active reads number A + 1 (for example 13) and Inactive number B.
  - Step 5:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 6:
    - Active reads number A (back to the starting number) and Inactive number B + 1.
  - Step 7:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 8:
    - Active number A − 1, Inactive number B + 2.
  - Step 9:
    - Net 45 and term A are among its rows.
  - Step 10:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 11:
    - Active number A, Inactive number B + 1.
  - Step 12:
    - The menu opens.
  - Step 13:
    - The confirmation opens.
  - Step 14:
    - Active number A and Inactive number B: both counts are back where they started (for example 12 and 0).

- Title (old = new): The selected count follows search, Select All and Select None
- **Old preconditions**

  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - The shop's own term {Term-A}, due 20 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly five customers: {Customer-A}, {Customer-B}, {Customer-C}, {Customer-D} and {Customer-E}; only {Customer-B} and {Customer-D} have "Fleet" in their names. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 N20, they are ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 Alpha, ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 Bravo Fleet, ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 Charlie, ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 Delta Fleet and ZZAUTOTEST DATA-25 Echo._
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure the shop's own term A, due 20 days after the invoice date, is held by exactly five customers made for this test: customer A, customer B, customer D and two more; only customer B and customer D have "Fleet" in their names.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page (Settings, then "Credit Terms" in the Finance group), Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click the "⋯" at the right of the {Term-A} row.  →  Click the "⋯" at the right of the term A row.

It was used by invoices {Invoice-A} (paid), {Invoice-B} (reversed) and {Invoice-C} (open), and by an estimate {Estimate-A} that was never invoiced; the customer was then moved to Net 30. _For example: {Term-A} is ZZAUTOTEST DATA-27 Fourteen and the records belong to ZZAUTOTEST DATA-27 Customer._

### D-01 · C724157 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724157
- Title (old = new): Design: Credit Terms page and table look as designed
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop has every standard term switched on, and you compare the Credit Terms page with the design.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - All twelve standard terms are active and COD is the default for new customers.
  - The browser window is 1440 px wide, at 100% zoom. _For example: a maximised window on a 1440 x 900 screen._
  - The design pictures ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png, ct-p1-05-row-menu-a-standard-term.png are attached to this case; open each at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Starting screen: the Settings page.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure all twelve standard terms are active and COD is the default for new customers.
  - Use a desktop browser window 1440 pixels wide, at 100% zoom.
  - Open the attached design pictures ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png, ct-p1-05-row-menu-a-standard-term.png at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Start on the Settings page.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  1. In the Settings menu, click "Credit Terms".
  2. Resize the browser window to 1536 px wide (for example, maximise it on a 1536 x 864 screen at 100% zoom).
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the page matches the attached picture ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png: in the side menu the Finance group shows "Payment Methods" and, directly below it, "Credit Terms" with a clock icon, in blue because it is open. The title "Credit Terms" is at the top left; at the top right are a square outlined "⋯" button and, right of it, the only blue button on the page, "+ New Credit Term". Below are the "Active" and "Inactive" tabs, then one table (no sections, no card around it) with the headings Name, Due, Customers and Vendors and a last column with no heading. Customers and Vendors counts are right aligned and 0 shows as 0. Every row is one line and ends with a grey "⋯". Only COD, Due on Receipt and Credit Hold show an info (i) icon and a lock icon after the name. The heading style and the font: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here (ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png draws small grey headings, the build picture ct-p1-05-row-menu-a-standard-term.png bold black ones).
  - Step 2: at 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll; the layout described above is unchanged, only wider.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - The page matches the attached picture ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png: in the side menu the Finance group shows "Payment Methods" and, directly below it, "Credit Terms" with a clock icon, in blue because it is open.
    - The title "Credit Terms" is at the top left.
    - At the top right are a square outlined "⋯" button and, right of it, the only blue button on the page, "+ New Credit Term".
    - Below are the "Active" and "Inactive" tabs, then one table (no sections, no card around it) with the headings Name, Due, Customers and Vendors and a last column with no heading.
    - Customers and Vendors counts are right aligned and 0 shows as 0.
    - Every row is one line and ends with a grey "⋯".
    - Only COD, Due on Receipt and Credit Hold show an info (i) icon and a lock icon after the name.
    - The heading style and the font: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here (ct-p1-01-active-tab-one-table.png draws small grey headings, the build picture ct-p1-05-row-menu-a-standard-term.png bold black ones).
  - Step 2:
    - At 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll.
    - The layout described above is unchanged, only wider.

### D-13 · C724175 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724175
- Title (old = new): Design: New Credit Term window with Net days looks as designed
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: You start a new term due 14 days after the invoice date and compare the window with the design.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Today on the calendar of the location you work in is {Date-A}, and {Date-B} is the date 14 days later. _For example: {Date-A} is Oct 21 and {Date-B} is Nov 4._
  - The browser window is 1440 px wide, at 100% zoom. _For example: a maximised window on a 1440 x 900 screen._
  - The design picture ct-p3-01-new-credit-term-net-x-days.png is attached to this case; open it at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page, Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Note today's date on the calendar of the location you work in (date A).
  - Work out date B: the date 14 days later.
  - Use a desktop browser window 1440 pixels wide, at 100% zoom.
  - Open the attached design picture ct-p3-01-new-credit-term-net-x-days.png at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page, Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **Old steps** / **new steps**
  1. Click "New Credit Term".
  2. Type "Net 14 Days" in Name.
  3. Type 14 in the Days box.
  4. Resize the browser window to 1536 px wide (for example, maximise it on a 1536 x 864 screen at 100% zoom).
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: a window titled "New Credit Term" opens with a close × at the right.
  - Step 2: Name reads "Net 14 Days".
  - Step 3: the window matches ct-p3-01-new-credit-term-net-x-days.png: the Name box with its floating label and the grey hint "Up to 31 characters. Prints on invoices and goes to QuickBooks." under it; below, two rows with round option buttons: "Due Net X days" (chosen) with the Days box and "days after the invoice date" after it, and "Due day of the following month" with a greyed, empty Day box and pale "of the following month"; under them a light-blue box with an info icon and blue text "Net 14 Days means payment is due 14 days after the invoice date. An invoice dated {Date-A} is due {Date-B}."; at the bottom right "Cancel" (white) and "Create Credit Term" (blue). The Cancel style: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here.
  - Step 4: at 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll; the layout described above is unchanged, only wider.
- **New Expected Result**
  - Step 1:
    - A window titled "New Credit Term" opens with a close × at the right.
  - Step 2:
    - Name reads "Net 14 Days".
  - Step 3:
    - The window matches ct-p3-01-new-credit-term-net-x-days.png: the Name box with its floating label and the grey hint "Up to 31 characters. Prints on invoices and goes to QuickBooks." under it.
    - Below, two rows with round option buttons: "Due Net X days" (chosen) with the Days box and "days after the invoice date" after it, and "Due day of the following month" with a greyed, empty Day box and pale "of the following month"
    - Under them a light-blue box with an info icon and blue text "Net 14 Days means payment is due 14 days after the invoice date. An invoice dated date A is due date B."
    - At the bottom right "Cancel" (white) and "Create Credit Term" (blue).
    - The Cancel style: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here.
  - Step 4:
    - At 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll.
    - The layout described above is unchanged, only wider.

### D-17 · C724179 · https://shopview.testrail.io/index.php?/cases/view/724179
- Title (old = new): Design: Move Customers window looks as designed
- **Old preconditions**
  - Scenario: Your shop uses Due on Receipt as the default and has many customers on COD, and you compare Move Customers with the design.
  - You are an Admin user working at {Location-A}. _For example: {Location-A} is Staging Heavy Duty - 9919._
  - Due on Receipt is the default for new customers, not COD.
  - At least ten customers are on COD, among them {Customer-A} and {Customer-B}. _For example: {Customer-A} is ZZAUTOTEST D-17 Alpha and {Customer-B} is ZZAUTOTEST D-17 Bravo._
  - The browser window is 1440 px wide, at 100% zoom. _For example: a maximised window on a 1440 x 900 screen._
  - The design pictures ct-p2-02-move-customers-from-cod.png, ct-p2-03-some-customers-unticked.png, ct-p2-06-move-to-cod-the-0-line.png are attached to this case; open each at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Starting screen: the Credit Terms page, Active tab.
  - If any record above is missing, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".
- **New preconditions**
  - Sign in as an Admin at the test location.
  - Make sure Due on Receipt is the default for new customers, not COD.
  - Make sure at least ten customers are on COD, among them customer A.
  - Use a desktop browser window 1440 pixels wide, at 100% zoom.
  - Open the attached design pictures ct-p2-02-move-customers-from-cod.png, ct-p2-03-some-customers-unticked.png, ct-p2-06-move-to-cod-the-0-line.png at 100% zoom beside the screen.
  - Start on the Credit Terms page, Active tab.
  - If you cannot set up any of the above, mark this test Blocked: "setup not done".

  1. Click "⋯" on the COD row.
  2. Click "Move Customers".
  3. Untick {Customer-A}.  →  Untick customer A.
  4. Resize the browser window to 1536 px wide (for example, maximise it on a 1536 x 864 screen at 100% zoom).
- **Old results**
  - Step 1: the row menu opens.
  - Step 2: a window titled "Move Customers from COD" opens (as in ct-p2-02-move-customers-from-cod.png). Top to bottom: a "Search customers" box with a magnifier; blue "Select All" and "Select None" at the left and "{n} selected" in grey at the right; a bordered scrolling list, one customer per row with a line between rows, A to Z, every box ticked; the "Move To" box reading "Due on Receipt" with the grey line "Prefilled with your default for new customers." under it; at the bottom right "Cancel" and a blue "Move {n} Customers". The ticked-box style (light blue with a blue tick in the drawn picture, solid blue with a white tick in the build picture ct-p2-06-move-to-cod-the-0-line.png), the Move To box style (outlined with a small arrow, or filled with a solid arrow) and the Cancel style: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here.
  - Step 3: the box of {Customer-A} is empty, the count reads one less and the button reads "Move" with the lower number (as in ct-p2-03-some-customers-unticked.png).
  - Step 4: at 1536 px nothing overlaps, nothing is cut off and nothing needs a sideways scroll; the layout described above is unchanged, only wider.

    - The row menu opens.
  - Step 2:
    - A window titled "Move Customers from COD" opens (as in ct-p2-02-move-customers-from-cod.png).
    - Top to bottom: a "Search customers" box with a magnifier.
    - Blue "Select All" and "Select None" at the left and "N selected" in grey at the right.
    - A bordered scrolling list, one customer per row with a line between rows, A to Z, every box ticked.
    - The "Move To" box reading "Due on Receipt" with the grey line "Prefilled with your default for new customers." under it.
    - At the bottom right "Cancel" and a blue "Move N Customers".
    - The ticked-box style (light blue with a blue tick in the drawn picture, solid blue with a white tick in the build picture ct-p2-06-move-to-cod-the-0-line.png), the Move To box style (outlined with a small arrow, or filled with a solid arrow) and the Cancel style: this attribute depends on question 12 (drawn pictures or build pictures, not answered yet): for now compare it with the picture named here.

## 4. No-fact-lost exceptions
- S4-04: '$0.00', '$1,000.00', '$500.00', '0.00', '00', '1,000', '500.00' — dropped in the relevance pass with a record no step or result uses (the three customers' credit limits and A to Z order; see RELEVANCE-PASS.md)
- S4-08: '$0.00' — dropped in the relevance pass with a record no step or result uses (the third customer's $0.00 credit limit; see RELEVANCE-PASS.md)
- S4-16: '$0.00', '$1,000.00', '$500.00', '0.00', '00', '1,000', '500.00' — dropped in the relevance pass with a record no step or result uses (the three customers' credit limits and A to Z order; see RELEVANCE-PASS.md)
- S4-20: '$0.00', '$1,000.00', '$500.00', '0.00', '00', '1,000', '500.00' — dropped in the relevance pass with a record no step or result uses (the three customers' credit limits and A to Z order; see RELEVANCE-PASS.md)
- S4-34: '$0.00', '$1,000.00', '$500.00', '0.00', '00', '1,000', '500.00' — dropped in the relevance pass with a record no step or result uses (the three customers' credit limits and A to Z order; see RELEVANCE-PASS.md)
- D-33: 'Invoice: INV-work order 1', 'Terms term A', 'Work Order work order 1' — same facts, new words (hand-written result, section 2): the on-screen labels joined the work order number and term name to placeholders ("INV-{Work-order-1}", "Work Order {Work-order-1}", "Terms {Term-A}"); they now read "INV-" followed by work order 1's number, "Work Order" with work order 1's number, and "Terms" with term A's name.

Facts that were only in the removed Scenario line:
- S4-11: '30', 'Net 30' — the removed Scenario line said the customer moves to Net 30; the case's own steps and results move it to Due on Receipt (the default) and still do. The Scenario line was wrong; nothing in the case changes.
- S5-25: '$500' — the Scenario's "$500" is the step's "credit limit of 500", still in the step.
- S9-04: '$600,' — the Scenario's "$600" is the precondition's "$600.00", still in it.

## 5. Cases that could not be reformatted without losing meaning
None. Every case is in the pattern; the few bullets the rules could not phrase plainly were written by hand (section 2) with the same meaning.
